DRAIN review (ses_e22f5669cffeB92YnnuXa7V0S4, conn-drain-review.md; own worktree at e77e2787, removed; ran gates 61 summaries / 326 passed / 5 ignored / no FAILED, clippy Finished, 30/30 loops, size gate 5,496, allocation probe 0): REQUEST CHANGES, one Important. `timeout_at` polls the drain future before its timer, so a drain task FIRST scheduled after its 100 ms deadline (executor stalled 150 ms, terminator already readable) still checks the connection in (pool.rs:230–237, body.rs:203–209; reviewer's `review_expired_before_first_poll`: expected idle 0, got 1, twice). Everything else held under the reviewer's own probes: late terminator + forged response in one write → closed; forged response after check-in before checkout → rejected; chunk extensions, trailers, split size line, byte-by-byte terminator → reused; 60,000 post-End bytes reused, 66,000 closed; slow drip closed near 100 ms; 32 + 32 capacity recovery; no wrapper budget leaking into the next response (100,000-byte second response fine); no reference cycle; request-path read functions unchanged apart from a new `Finishing` match arm on the connection type. Reverting adoption makes 4 tests fail. Report error: seven drain unit tests, not eight. Missing committed cases: expired-before-first-poll, slow drip, capacity recovery, runtime teardown, excess initial buffer, non-reusable hand-off.
Controller reading of the reviewer's "after-checkout poisoning" probe: the origin waits for request two on the reused socket and then answers it with a forged body — that is the upstream answering the request it was sent; a connection is only ever reused for the SAME scheme/host/port, and an origin can answer any request with anything, reused socket or not. Not a defect and not widened in kind by the drain; recorded so nobody claims more than "a connection is returned only at a message boundary with nothing buffered, and a peer that sends unsolicited bytes before the next checkout is rejected".
Reviewer on the C preload: acceptable only as an OPTIONAL Linux diagnostic, not a guarantee; it moves unsafe native code elsewhere rather than removing it; silent when not loaded. Suggests a source-structural guard in ordinary `cargo test` (no boxing in the request-path read functions). Ruling: the structural guard is added now (no unsafe, runs everywhere); whether the C file stays as an optional diagnostic or is deleted remains the user's decision (asked, unanswered) — the branch is not merged with it until they answer.
Fix round 1 → same implementer (turn 4, narrow): deadline checked before draining and before check-in + regression; the missing committed cases; structural guard; report correction.

CONTROLLER INFERENCE WAS WRONG: the ledger said "~35 ms of the Rust request is outside the function". Measured inside the release gateway (scratch instrumentation, nine requests): auth 0.13 ms, reader acquisition 0.13, blocking queue 0.02, `history` 67.6, response construction 0.002 → handler 68.1 ms; client-observed minus handler ≈ 7 ms. The function is slow IN THE GATEWAY, not the transport. Cause of the mismatch with the 43 ms benchmark: the benchmark's sample table was a rowid table; production is `WITHOUT ROWID` with primary key (credential_id, window_type, observed_at) and a secondary index on observed_at, so every row found through the index costs a seek into the primary-key tree. Standalone on the production schema, file-backed: 58.7 ms (47.4 with only the table layout changed back); the remaining ~9 ms to the in-gateway 67.6 is unattributed (different runs, allocator — gateway uses mimalloc, test binary does not —, WAL/cache state).
Frozen side measured the same way (actual createStore + accountQuotaHistory on a file database, Bun): function 40.8 ms + JSON.stringify 12.4 = 53.2 ms.
Transport: Rust writes the response with one writev (146-byte head + 8,550,404-byte body), TS with sendmsg + sendto; both close within ~2 ms of the write; no delayed close, no copy of the body in Rust.
End to end, untraced, nine each, load ~3.4–4.4: TS 62.0–92.0 (median 70.3), Rust 67.3–82.9 (median 75.1). The ranges overlap; Rust is not shown faster and is likely a few ms slower on this request.
State of the requirement on this route: at the 50,002-sample cap Rust went from ~2.5× slower to roughly level (−5 ms at the median on a noisy host); "faster" is not demonstrated. Smaller sizes were never compared against the frozen gateway.
Next: one more round with a FRESH session (this one has had three turns), measured on the production schema only: split of the function on a WITHOUT ROWID file database, then the Rust-side overhead above SQLite stepping (cell handling in the reader, the preliminary sort, serialisation); plus frozen-vs-Rust at 1,000 and 10,000 samples. If Rust is still not clearly faster after that, the numbers go to the user as they are for a decision on A8.

DRAIN RE-REVIEW 1 (ses_e22f5669cffeB92YnnuXa7V0S4, conn-drain-rereview.md, 548aed49; own worktree, removed): REQUEST CHANGES, deadline finding PARTIALLY addressed. No Critical. Important 2: the structural guard misses `Box::<_>::pin`, `Arc::new`, populated `vec![]`, `.to_string()`, `Bytes::copy_from_slice`, a same-file helper that boxes, and a `// }` comment ends the scanned body early (catches Box::pin, std::boxed::Box::pin, .boxed(), String::from). Also noted: the slow-drip case does not assert WHEN the connection closes, so a reset-on-byte timeout would pass; report sentence "can no longer cause a late check-in" too absolute. Confirmed by reviewer: original reproduction fixed (reverting the checks fails the committed regression); request-path functions byte-identical in range; size gate 5,496; removal of the preload complete; gates 61 summaries / 332 passed / 5 ignored / no FAILED, clippy Finished, loop 30/30.
Ruling: the guarantee is worded as an ADMISSION check — "a drained connection is inserted into the idle set only if the deadline has not passed at the moment of insertion, checked under the idle lock" — not as real-time closure. Ruling: the guard is tightened as far as a text scanner honestly goes (comment/string-aware body extraction, whitespace and turbofish normalised, wider deny list, every same-file function called from a scanned function must itself be scanned, scanner mutation tests committed); no parser dependency is added for it. What it cannot see stays stated in the file header and the report.

B3 DECODE-SIDE REVIEW (ses_e22a47338ffebWTmG23GwMJjDq, b3-decode-review.md, b21aee95; own worktree, removed): REQUEST CHANGES, no Critical. 310 own streams / 1,236 stream×chunking runs; 12 streams (48 runs) differ, in two classes. Important 1: a JSON number outside the double range (`1e309`) makes omni_ir::deep::parse fail, so the WHOLE event is skipped — `{"delta":"keep me","ignored":1e309}` loses the text; on `response.completed` the stream ends as retryable "ended before response completion" where frozen ends normally with Infinity. Important 2: `String(x)` on an object carrying an own `toString` key throws TypeError in JS (frozen adapter → "openai codec decode threw", retryable UPSTREAM); Rust's shared js::to_string hardcodes "[object Object]" and continues. Related: a ~40,000-level nested array as a delta makes the frozen join throw RangeError; Rust emits the joined text. Confirmed equal by the reviewer: event-type table (handled / ignored / unknown), all four reasoning × native-flag combinations, OAuth mode reads nothing on the decode side (16 own cases), invalid UTF-8, record cap just below/above, finish() without terminal, four 40,000-level probes on 2 MiB threads survive; decoder returns Continue at every terminal event (as the other two decoders do; the main-line adapter fix ends the body at End). Gate rerun 53 summaries / 236 passed / 4 ignored / no FAILED; clippy Finished.
Controller reading: both classes sit in SHARED code (omni_ir::deep::parse, omni_ir::js::to_string) that the merged custom and Anthropic decoders use too (custom/decode/mod.rs:141 json_object, anthropic/decode.rs:126; js::to_string at 13 custom sites) — by reading they have the same two differences on main; not yet observed.
Ruling (B3 decode, coercion throws): FIXED, not parked — the rule is exact and cheap: with JSON-born values, ToString/ToNumber throws iff an object with an own `toString` key is reached (directly, or as an element while joining an array). A fallible sibling of js::to_string / js::to_number in omni-ir; OpenAI decoder sites map the throw to DecodeError::Throw (adapter already relabels it as the frozen "codec decode threw"). The custom and Anthropic decoders get the same treatment in a separate commit AFTER a frozen-vs-Rust row shows the same difference there.
Ruling (B3 decode, out-of-range numbers): PARKED under roadmap X1 as a third class next to lone surrogates and non-numeric usage — serde_json::Value cannot hold ±Infinity, the lossless carrier X1 needs (jsjson::parse_js already yields ±Infinity) is the fix; no Rust-only lossy rewrite is added meanwhile. Ignored test + conformance note with the reviewer's six streams; one observed row each for the custom and Anthropic decoders. Goes on the user's known-difference list: "an upstream event containing a number beyond the double range is dropped whole".
Ruling (B3 decode, RangeError on deep array join): documented difference — the frozen threshold is the JS engine's stack depth, not a contract; Rust returns the joined text. Record the measured frozen threshold once.

DRAIN RE-REVIEW 2 (ses_e22f5669cffeB92YnnuXa7V0S4, conn-drain-rereview2.md, 570d10ff; own worktree, removed): REQUEST CHANGES, no Critical, two Important. (1) The controller's reading is CONFIRMED by a test: `checkin_before` (pool.rs:331–341) pops the oldest idle connection to make room BEFORE the deadline check, so an expired drain evicts a healthy idle connection and is then refused — per-destination limit 1: idle 1 → 0, the old peer sees EOF (r4/eviction.log). Fix: check the deadline first, insert, then evict while over the limit. (2) Scanner: a turbofish call to a same-file helper (`allocation_helper::<u8>()`) is not seen as a call — helper closure bypassed, all three tripwire tests pass (r4/generic-helper.log). Everything else in its second mutation matrix caught (closures, nested blocks, match arms, inner fn/impl, rebinding, raw strings, byte chars, lifetimes, Self:: calls, duplicate fn names); documented misses: braces in const-generic expressions before the body; one documented false-positive class (name-only match). Minor: 1 s lock test acceptable; 1,000 bounded yields in the paused-clock drip test is an acknowledged flake risk.

B3 INTEGRATION REVIEW at bcde5fee (ses_e21fd0d02ffeumtVMapTZbY9e6, b3-integration-review.md): REQUEST CHANGES, two findings, both verified by the controller against the code. (1) openai/oauth_lifecycle.rs claims() parses the ID-token payload with js::parse_json (serde_json::from_str, recursion limit 128); frozen decodeClaims (openai/oauth.ts:70–96) uses JSON.parse. Payload with email/account plus an unrelated array nested 200 deep: TS accountEmail=mail accountId=acct, Rust email None / accountId null (claim-{ts,rust}.log) — silent identity loss on exchange/reconnect/refresh. (2) oauth.rs FlowResult derives Clone and has default Drop over provider_data: serde_json::Map; 40,000 nested arrays on a 2 MiB thread abort on both clone and drop (depth-{clone,drop}.log: stack overflow, exit -6). Reviewer notes current OpenAI results are shallow, so this is the carrier contract, not a demonstrated remote exploit; inherited store Credential derive explicitly not demanded. Reviewer gates, controller-scanned: workspace 183 summaries / 1,247 passed / 19 ignored, zero failure markers; clippy, fmt exit 0; size 5,496; 56 HTTP rows failed 0; 35 bench tests; 48 smoke rows; live PG probe 1 passed through provider result → Store.save_oauth → Refresher (HTTP URL rewrite, not TLS gateway / cluster). Reviewer's own additions all equal: token 401/400/429/503/malformed (5 rows), 503→success retry, strengthened CAS fixture; mutation probe fails as it should (3 rows, 1 failed). Limits it states: PG CAS loser / identity backfill / malformed response and TS PG writes not run; 429 success after cooldown not exercised; no deep native-disconnect OpenAI case; non-null winning idToken untested in the HTTP race; copied HTTP binary provenance rests partly on handoff notes; JSON inventory is lexical and missed finding 2; EPOCHREALTIME needs Bash 5+ and is wall-clock. Scratch worktree and PG container removed (controller checked: neither present).

Superseded failures retained and read by the controller: focused.log/workspace.log = compile error E0509 (moves out of a Drop type in anthropic_token.rs, fixed by 0ee91d31); focused-final.log/workspace-final.log = jsjson_vectors and omni-coord scripts tests panicking NotFound on CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR fixture paths — implementer attributes this to cached test executables built from the reviewer's removed scratch checkout in the shared target dir and forced a rebuild by touching mtimes (worktree still shows only the report dirty). Controller lesson (new instance of an old one): a reviewer scratch sharing an implementer's target dir can leave test binaries pointing at a deleted checkout — and, worse, a later scratch could silently read another checkout's fixtures; reviewers must now prove their binaries were compiled from their own scratch. CLI POST-MERGE DRIVERS on the combined tree (binaries built at 2c372c3d): run.ts exit 0, all rows equal. CLI matrix: 38/47 exit 0, NINE FAIL (status, plugin-refusals, keys-list, model-reads, model-removals, model-puts, model-catalog, config-gate, status-rows). E1/E5 NOT ticked. Root causes isolated by the controller (systematic-debugging; logs /tmp/opencode/cli-merge/matrix-*.log, noenv-*.log, isolate-*.log):
(A) CONTROLLER ERROR, harness environment — 2 suites (status, plugin-refusals). The matrix was run from the MAIN checkout, which has a real `.env`; Bun auto-loads it from cwd (probe: main checkout OMNI_DB_PATH/OMNI_PORT/OMNI_ENCRYPTION_KEY/OMNI_DAY_OFFSET_MINUTES all SET, rust-cli worktree all unset — it has no .env). The drivers' own bun process, and every TS CLI child a driver starts without `--no-env-file` (only 42 of 66 conformance/cli files carry the flag), therefore inherited the user's real OMNI_DB_PATH=/home/homebrew/.config/omnigateway/omnigateway.db and real encryption key. Symptom: Rust prints "ignoring OMNI_DB_PATH=… because --root was given" into compared output. Rerun with `bun --no-env-file`: status exit 0, plugin-refusals exit 0. Damage check: the real database has mtime AND ctime 2026-10-23 22:24:10 (ctime cannot be set from user space), its directory mtime 2026-10-23, no -wal/-shm, nothing listening on :9000, no stray driver processes; sha256 recorded afterwards 354aa4c5… (no before-hash exists); the real key string appears in zero files under /tmp/opencode/cli-merge and zero files created in temp roots during the run. So: no write to the real installation observed, but the run violated the standing rule "temporary roots only, never real installations" in spirit — every earlier matrix ran from a worktree without .env. run.ts spreads process.env into both gateways and overrides DB path/key/host/port, so it also carried the user's .env profile overrides (identically on both sides; same as every earlier run.ts from the main checkout).
(B) REAL REGRESSION from the merge, semantic conflict without a textual one — 7 suites (keys-list, model-reads, model-removals, model-puts, model-catalog, config-gate which wraps model-puts, status-rows). Main's Nov-14 log-redaction commits (19e6cc27, ab409566, a95494b3; 9cbd3b8c) made StoreError::StoredJson Display print "unreadable row: <table>.<column> of <id>: not valid JSON" with the parser text only reachable through ParserDetail::into_wire_detail, and made control credentials::provider_data fail with `invalid credential provider_data: id="q"`. The CLI branch (forked at 9624fa13, before those commits; it has no StoredJson at all) renders these errors with `e.to_string()` / `e.message` and its goldens expect the frozen TS text (`JSON Parse error: Unterminated string`, `… Unexpected identifier "broken"`, `… Expected '}'`). Isolation, same checkout, same drivers, no .env: pre-merge branch binary db22-omni-final → keys-list, model-reads, status-rows all exit 0; merged binary → all seven exit 1 with exactly those redacted texts. Workspace cargo tests cannot see this (the CLI contract lives in the bun drivers), which is why the post-merge Rust gates were green. The reviewer's prerequisite "retain main StoredJson handling" was followed; neither it nor the controller checked how the CLI renders that error.
Controller lessons: (1) run CLI/e2e drivers only from a checkout without `.env`, or with `bun --no-env-file` and an explicit scrubbed environment; check `env | grep OMNI` equivalents before the first driver; (2) "no textual conflict" is not "no semantic conflict" — when one side changes a Display/error text and the other side prints errors, grep the other side's render sites before merging; (3) post-merge Rust gates do not certify the CLI; the driver matrix is part of the merge gate.

CLI STORED-JSON REVIEW at 21348e06 (ses_e21a35811ffeXjS4omnv8GgAfI, cli-stored-json-review.md): REQUEST CHANGES. Reviewer ran 1,045 paired TS/Rust commands (five columns × eleven cell classes × keys/models/setup/status/settings commands, tables recreated without affinity): 904 equal, 141 different (/tmp/opencode/cli-fix-review/all-differences.{md,json}, all-pairs.json). Controller tallied the 141 from the JSON: BLOB 54, INTEGER/REAL 54, NULL 17, TEXT classes 16; api_keys.limits 40, virtual_models.targets 39, api_keys.model_allowlist 36, credentials.provider_data 26. Three Important, all verified by the controller against the code: (F1, in the new code) credentials::cli_list parses raw bytes — INTEGER/REAL cell: TS status exit 0, Rust `Invalid type` exit 1; BLOB `{}`: TS `JSON Parse error: Unable to parse JSON string` exit 1, Rust exit 0 (frozen JSON.parse applies ToString to the bun:sqlite value first); (F2, pre-existing) setup.rs:287–308 private query with `.ok()` swallows bad provider_data so both setups reach the model prompt where TS stops with the parser message (frozen setup.ts:57–58 lists every credential first); (F3, pre-existing — row.rs unchanged since the CLI branch) strict typed operator reads (row.rs:16–30, store models.rs:41, store keys.rs:434–435) make keys/models/setup commands differ for NULL/INTEGER/REAL/BLOB cells, for invalid-UTF-8 TEXT in mutation summaries (TS succeeds), and for `targets={}` (TS `{} is not iterable`). Two Minor: env guard names only two variables (a .env with only OMNI_PORT=invalid turns status into an assertion failure, not a refusal); driver_env.py leaves an untracked __pycache__. What holds, from the reviewer's own build (compilation provenance proven): workspace 185 summaries / 1,265 passed / 19 ignored; clippy; fmt; matrix 48/48 once; run.ts equal; admin-routes one attempt alone 20,602 equal / 27 expected / 0 failures; gateway boot with a canary in broken JSON → zero canary hits in output, 106 Rust log streams clean; project_keys/cli_error reachable from CLI only; all top-level drivers guarded, the 11 unguarded files are helpers/seams/child wrappers; every bun child passes --no-env-file. Controller checked the reviewer's exit files and logs (workspace totals, matrix 48 exit 0, e2e, admin-routes; binary sha256 fa4e8a49… equals the implementer's). Not established by anyone: how many of the 141 already differed before this fix (not rerun against the pre-fix binary).
Consequence for earlier claims: the CLI approvals for status/keys/models/setup (and the 46/47/48-suite matrices) never exercised non-TEXT, NULL or invalid-UTF-8 stored cells outside the `db` commands; those approvals stand only for what their suites covered. e2e drivers (run.ts, admin-routes.ts, openai.ts) still spread process.env and are unguarded — flagged, not part of this fix.
Ruling: fix all four classes, including INTEGER/REAL/NULL cells that need a hand-altered table — same standard as the earlier rulings for clear-bodies non-TEXT paths and migration-ledger ids (frozen cell semantics, raw bun:sqlite values). Gateway-side typed readers (row::text etc.) must NOT be loosened; operator-only readers beside them. If a class needs open-ended JS emulation, the implementer stops at a clean commit and reports rather than masking. The branch is NOT merged while under request-changes, so main keeps the seven failing CLI suites until this lands.

PARALLEL TRACKS (user asked what can run in parallel). Host budget checked: 8 cores, load ~1.1, 10 GiB RAM available, 4.2 GiB disk free, two build caches (main target 7.2 GiB idle, rust-cli target 4.9 GiB in use by the CLI fix). Decision: two build tracks + one no-build track; a third build track is refused by disk (a fresh target dir costs 4–6 GiB) until space is freed.
(1) CLI fix round 2 — running (above), rust-cli worktree + its own target.
(2) A15 /api/connect/* — fresh implementer ses_e21698c8cffex9TNrjT5mAJxGv, rust-providers worktree on new branch feat/rust-connect-routes at 665ca7e5 (clean, no .env, zero OMNI_* visible), CARGO_TARGET_DIR lent = main checkout's target (idle; build artefacts only; mandatory mtime touch first). Chosen because its prerequisites just merged (B1 framework, B3 lifecycle, Store::save_oauth proven on live PG) and its files do not overlap the CLI fix: brief forbids edits to omni-cli, control {credentials,models,keys,cli_error}.rs, store {row,models,keys}.rs and conformance/cli; new logic in new files. Scope rulings in the brief: anthropic + openai only; plugin flows out (plugins unsupported); device machinery only for kinds the ported providers have; pending flows through coord kv with the frozen key/TTL, in-memory until D4 wiring; must persist through Store::save_oauth; red-first differential driver conformance/e2e/connect.ts with explicit environments and --no-env-file. Evidence /tmp/opencode/a15/, report a15-report.md in its worktree.
(3) X3 reproduction — read-only agent ses_e21698c89ffeDLkIvOHSbeYH1r, NO cargo: paired HTTP probe of the frozen binary vs /tmp/opencode/b3-merge/omnigateway-049e24c2 over admin/client routes with malformed id-like cells (the X3 suspects) and the five JSON columns from the CLI review, ports 19500–19599, work dir /tmp/opencode/x3-probe/, report x3-probe.md. Purpose: turn X3 from "suspected by reading" into measured evidence, and tell whether the HTTP side shares the CLI's stored-cell gap.
Consequences accepted: the controller cannot run cargo in the main checkout while track 2 holds its target dir (post-merge gates wait for it); admin-routes.ts wall-clock gates may fail under the combined load — implementers were told to record such a failure and the controller reruns alone before any merge; merge order CLI fix → A15 (or reverse) needs the semantic pre-check learned today.

B4 FINDING (request side, open): a 40,000-level nested toolUse.input makes Rust build_request overflow a 2 MiB stack (process abort), where frozen throws RangeError from JSON.stringify. Controller analysis: antigravity build_request does not run inside omni_ir::deep::scope as anthropic/codec.rs and openai/mod.rs do; and the test nests 40,000 levels inside the input, i.e. deeper than deep::MAX_DEPTH once the envelope wraps it — a depth ingress never delivers. Controller measured frozen (Bun 1.4.4, from the rust-bench worktree with --no-env-file, bisected twice with identical results; scripts /tmp/opencode/b4/stringify-depth{,2}.ts): bare JSON.stringify handles 40,000 nested objects/arrays; the Antigravity envelope first throws at 39,994 levels in toolUse.input (39,993 ok) and at 39,999 in vendor.antigravity.extra (39,998 ok).
Ruling: (B4 depth) Rust must never overflow for any value the IR can carry; where frozen serialises, bytes must be equal (tests at 39,990 levels on a 2 MiB thread, the openai_depth.rs convention); in the narrow band where frozen's JSON.stringify throws RangeError but the value is inside Rust's depth bound, Rust serialising successfully is a documented difference (engine stack threshold) — consistent with the B3 ruling on deep array join and the ingress TOO_DEEP difference. Not emulated.
Controller action: fresh implementer ses_e213ae47effeW4qfk0DXcNUO2o (same worktree at 5e7b0e2a): Part 1 fix the overflow (scope + deep helpers, green 39,990 tests for input/vendor/schema/response-format, one band test, mutation check), Part 2 decode.rs from frozen decode.ts with the controller's reading of its JS-semantics traps (response:null and failure:null throw; arrays pass the object check; Object.hasOwn lookups; functionCall with non-string name falls through to the text check; String() coercion sites; guardStream outcome) and then `impl ProviderCodec`; evidence /tmp/opencode/b4/turn4/. B4 request fixes (ses_e210eae4dffeGPxXUCITfdnPN3) stopped before any change on the I1 contract question, as instructed: evidence /tmp/opencode/b4/turn6/i1-contract-blocker.txt. Controller confirmed in code: `ProviderCodec::build_request` returns only `Result<_, GatewayError>` (codec.rs:158); `adapter::send` passes every build error through `rebound` (adapter.rs:168), which by frozen design drops gateway_authored; frozen codecAdapter.ts guard (173–193) maps an ordinary throw to `codecFailure(id, "buildRequest", "threw")` = UPSTREAM, `<id> codec buildRequest threw`, provider set, gatewayAuthored — and the Rust `codec_failure` helper (adapter.rs:85) is only reachable for decode throws and unusable requests. So a frozen build-hook throw (null inputSchema → Object.entries TypeError) cannot be represented today; no sibling codec needed it.
Ruling: (B4 I1 contract) add the build-side carrier additively: `BuildError { Gateway(GatewayError), Throw }` in omni-providers codec.rs with a PROVIDED trait method `try_build_request` defaulting to `build_request().map_err(Gateway)`; adapter.rs:168 calls it and maps `Throw` to `codec_failure(id, "buildRequest", "threw")`; sibling codecs untouched; Antigravity overrides it. Required proof: adapter-level test (null schema → UPSTREAM `antigravity codec buildRequest threw`, provider antigravity, gateway_authored true, no HTTP request; missing token → AUTH with gateway_authored false), sibling corpora green untouched, and the dispatch_size gate (5,496 / ceiling 5,500, never raised — adapter.rs is on the request path; if it exceeds, a non-generic inline(never) mapping helper, else stop and report). This widens the B4 branch's file set by codec.rs and adapter.rs (two shared provider files): to watch at merge time against the A15 branch (touches openai/{mod,oauth,profile}.rs only) and the CLI branch (no provider files).

CONTROLLER FINDING on 1f32b17c (not reported by the implementer): `schema::prune` is now `try_prune(node).expect(...)` and wire.rs still calls it for every tool schema; safety against a null root rests on a pre-scan in codec.rs that duplicates wire.rs's tool selection (portable, cloaked name, duplicate skipping). The release profile is panic = "abort", so any drift between the two, or a direct call of the public to_wire/prune with a null schema, would kill the gateway process on client input. To be fixed structurally: to_wire fallible with try_prune at the frozen call site, pre-scan deleted, no expect on client data.
B4 decode-side independent review (ses_e210eae4bffeuQJRa19M31EzJs, detached 08683466 in rust-postgres, report copied to b4-decode-review.md, evidence /tmp/opencode/b4-decode-review/): REQUEST CHANGES. Own fuzz 22,061 streams = 22,025 equal / 36 different (6 depth, 30 X1); 30 mutations = 29 detected, 1 equivalent survivor; gate 60 summaries / 240 passed / 0 failed / 12 ignored; checkout restored. The decoder logic matched frozen on every representable in-bound stream; line audit found no difference in `??`/undefined/null handling, table lookups, coercions, block indexing, usage (the 4th argument 0.0 is cacheWriteTokens, frozen's default), duplicate/`__proto__` keys, SSE feeding; the adapter does not call finish() after a Throw; client-visible result of a throw equals frozen's guardStream (`antigravity codec decode threw`, retryable UPSTREAM). Important: D1 a frame nested deeper than 40,000 is refused up front (Throw before any event of that frame) where frozen JSON.parse accepts it — in an ignored field, usageMetadata or error frozen continues/answers normally, and for deep args frozen emits start + tool blockStart before its stringify RangeError; D2 the X1 text test fails in the test transport (serde cannot read the oracle's lone-surrogate expectation) before reaching the decoder, and X1 positions are under-covered (thinking text, tool name/id, args key/string, responseId/modelVersion, error message/status, ignored fields, out-of-range numbers in usage/args/ignored). Minor: u32 block/tool counters would panic (debug) or wrap (release) after 2^32 blocks in one stream; commit 8aef0f19's subject says 2 green / 929 red where the run was 0 / 931, and the decoder audit table lacks several rows; six measured frozen billing fixtures are not in the corpus verbatim.
Ruling: (B4 decode D1) the up-front refusal of upstream frames deeper than the Rust JSON bound stays as a documented difference — it is the shared bounded parser every Rust decoder uses (the OpenAI decoder throws the same way; client bodies are bounded at ingress), and frozen parity would need an unbounded parser; it must be pinned by committed rows at depth 40,001 in the four positions and mid-stream, as an explicit counted expected-divergence class. Ruling: (B4 decode counters) u32 indices stay (shared IR type); exhaustion becomes a defined terminal UPSTREAM error via checked_add rather than panic/wrap; Rust-only bound, documented. Both to be listed for the user.

Closed: I1 (success, refusal, delayed transport close and 31 s timeout with the last waiter gone — consumed, one credential on success, none on failure), I3 (196-pair field × type × route matrix all equal), I4, M1. The implementer's unrequested refresh_token host check is CONFIRMED as frozen behaviour: pluginFlow.ts isFlowResult requires accessToken/refreshToken/apiKey/idToken null-or-string and an object providerData after the generator completes and before persistence; anthropic keeps a non-string refresh value until the host rejects it (502 `anthropic oauth exchange returned something the host cannot use`), openai normalises it to null first; step name is `exchange` for finish and poll alike. Measurements requested by the controller: held poll-versus-finish race is deterministic and EQUAL (two token requests, two credentials, two 200s on both sides — inherited frozen behaviour); SIGTERM 200 ms into a 1 s exchange with the client connected: all eight processes exit 0 with one persisted credential (TS 817–834 ms, Rust 815–822 ms). Visibility, 20 trials per side per provider: ordinary reconnect first request 20/20 on both sides; fresh connect frozen 20/20 first-request success, Rust 0/20 with lag 311–517 ms (inside the then-ruled 1 s bound).
NOT closed — R1 (Important): reconnect publication skips a credential that the held snapshot does not contain yet. connect.rs awaits SnapshotHolder::refresh_credential, but patch_credential_at_publish (omni-gateway-core snapshot.rs ~369–379) only replaces an existing entry; a row present in the DB but absent from the snapshot is ignored and Ok is returned. Reproduction: connect, immediately reconnect that credential, immediately request → frozen 200, Rust 503 `no eligible credential`; 0/20 on Rust vs 20/20 on frozen per provider via finish, same via poll (42 unexpected pairs). Controller confirmed in code. R2 (Minor): the new token-shape rows mask `body.id` to `<uuid>` without validating syntax or the persisted row.
Ruling: (A15 I2, REVISED — supersedes the earlier A15 I2 ruling) connect publishes its credential to the routing snapshot before acknowledging for BOTH a new credential and a reconnect, through an upsert publication; the `visibility-lag` expected-divergence class is removed from the connect driver (expected divergences back to 18) and the first request after a fresh connect must succeed on both sides. Reason for the revision: the reconnect-only rule left an edge the reviewer hit at once (a credential younger than one poll tick), an upsert closes that edge and the fresh-connect lag with the same mechanism, and the user's goal is frozen behaviour wherever it is cheap; the 500 ms lag of the OTHER admin config writes (A5/A6) is unchanged and remains documented. Cost accepted: the branch now touches omni-gateway-core snapshot.rs, whose W-then-P lock/read contract took three review rounds — so the change is additive (`publish_credential` beside the unchanged `refresh_credential`, same publication job, same authoritative read in the writer section; insert position = store listing order tier, label) with unit tests for insert position, replace, remove, read error, the unchanged non-inserting refresh path, and the delete/rebuild races. If Rust's first request after a fresh connect still fails for a reason outside publication, the implementer stops and reports rather than keeping a divergence class.

NOT closed — S1 (Important): the upsert replaces an existing entry IN PLACE even when its freshly read tier/label differ from the held row, so the list no longer follows the store order (`ORDER BY tier, label`), and list order is observable: omni-router filters.rs:240 enumerates credentials in list order and rank keeps arrival order for ties and weighted selection. Reproduction (both providers): two enabled equal-tier credentials labelled a and b; a's label is changed to z (SQLite writer or admin PATCH); immediately reconnect that credential; immediately request → frozen routes to b (now first), Rust to z (still at a's old position); 10/10 repeated admin-PATCH trials differ; after 600 ms the same tokens are still selected (the reviewer does not claim the array stayed unsorted — health/score feedback can preserve the effect). Controller confirmed in code (`*current = row.clone()` in publish_credential_at_read) and notes the new unit test pins the old position after a tier/label change, i.e. it asserts the defect. The same in-place replacement exists in refresh_credential since B1 and is out of scope here.
Ruling: (A15 S1) the connect upsert restores store order for its own row: when the fresh row's (tier, label) differs from the held entry's, remove it and insert at the sorted position (same comparator as the insert-missing case); when the key is unchanged, replace in place (keeps the tie position among equal keys). refresh_credential keeps replace-in-place unchanged. The unit test that pins the stale position is corrected to assert the store order. Driver gets the reviewer's changed-label row as a plain equal row, red first.

A15 fourth look (ses_e21357509ffennAt0Nyv1rXtOg; the session ended with a harness error `Bad Request` after writing a15-review4.md — the report (37 lines, copied to the main sdd dir) has findings but NO regression-sweep counts or gate section; several of its probe shells were still running in namespaces; evidence /tmp/opencode/a15-review4/): REQUEST CHANGES. The S1 example is fixed (tier change 80/80 equal, unchanged key 80/80, unchanged-equal 80/80; core tests and both gated race tests ×20 exit 0 per the `.exit` files) but store-order parity is still not exact: T1 (Important) a credential whose key changes to EQUAL another's is reinserted after all existing equal keys, whereas SQLite's `ORDER BY tier, label` leaves ties in rowid order — a lower-rowid credential moved into a newer neighbour's key lands on the wrong side: reviewer's `tie` (priority) 40 of 80 rows differ, `tie-rr` 40 of 80, `tie-weighted` 32 of 80; T2 (Important) labels are decoded lossily (row.rs from_utf8_lossy), so for an invalid-UTF-8 label the in-memory byte comparison differs from SQLite's raw-byte order (hand-altered metadata only; 2 of 4 rows differ, incl. one Rust 503). Minor: the new driver rows write the expected bearer to evidence but do not assert it. The reviewer's `order`, `order-admin`, `order-repeat` scripts exit 1 with half their rows failing — to be read with the reviewer's own caveat from round three that those scripts' pass marker encodes an expectation, not a TS/Rust comparison; not interpreted by the controller as S1 still failing without the missing counts section.
Controller assessment: two rounds of emulating the store's order in memory each left an edge (stale position → tie order → lossy bytes), because the snapshot does not hold what SQLite sorts on (rowid, raw bytes). The window is narrow — the difference exists only when the metadata change and the reconnect fall inside the same 500 ms poll tick, and the connect's own write triggers a full rebuild within the next tick — but the reviewer shows selection can stay different through health feedback. Options: (a) connect publishes by running the FULL snapshot rebuild before acknowledging (what the poll does ≤ 500 ms later anyway: store order, health, quota by construction; the upsert code and its tests come out again; SQLite path — the Postgres snapshot path is not wired yet); (b) patch the upsert again (carry rowid and raw label bytes into the comparison); (c) accept T1/T2 as documented differences. Findings: (1) malformed stored retention settings — frozen does JS arithmetic on the raw value ("oops" → NaN → nothing deleted; null/false → 0 days; true/"1" → 1 day), Rust's typed Settings substitutes 30 days, so Rust deletes data frozen keeps; (2) pending retirement via the bounded writer ignores send results/loss counters — full queue drops retirements and logs success (capacity 1, 3 dead rows: Rust completes 1, frozen 3); (3) the real gateway boot sweep in lib.rs:65–90 keeps a duplicated SELECT that zeroes RTK fields (pre-existing; A11's fix covered only the store helper). Also noted: shutdown waits maintenance with shutdown_until then gives HTTP a fresh deadline (drain can exceed one grace).
Ruling: (A11 settings) horizons computed from the raw stored setting with frozen JS semantics (ToNumber on scalars, NaN propagating, binding as bun:sqlite binds), typed Settings reader untouched; stop on object/array values. (A11 recovery) retirement must not lose work: guaranteed admission or direct completion transaction, actual counts logged, boot path too. (A11 boot RTK) shared reader, driver row with nonzero RTK. Shared shutdown deadline unless frozen orders otherwise. Documented differences recorded for the user: Coord bool → no `maintenance lease failed` line; tokio Skip vs Bun setInterval phase under stalls; spawn_blocking overlap; process-local MemoryCoord at boot. Sent to the A11 implementer (same session), evidence /tmp/opencode/a11/r1-*.

Recovery audit: BEGIN IMMEDIATE before load → no double rollup when the live writer wins first; one row per transaction as frozen; failure keeps earlier commits as frozen; busy_timeout 5 s as frozen; commit-sink omission matches frozen (frozen sweep calls store.complete directly — no limiter debit), though hourly own-node pending rows can belong to active requests (same predicate as frozen). REMAINING Important: the raw settings text is parsed with serde_json, so JSON.parse-valid overflow numbers (1e309 → Infinity) and lone-surrogate strings make Rust default the whole settings object to 30 days — 3 of 4 paired raw-text prunes differ (frozen deletes nothing for 1e309 / "\ud800", everything for -1e309; an unrelated bad key discards a valid logRetentionDays). This is the X1 carrier class on a deletion path, but a lossless parser exists.
Ruling: (A11 settings parse) use omni_ir::jsjson::parse_js (preserves infinities and lone surrogates) for the raw settings text; no serde roundtrip; red-first raw-text cases. Sent to the A11 implementer, evidence /tmp/opencode/a11/r2-*.

FINDING (Important): the controller's cache-warmth explanation of race/false/5xx/6 is refuted — both sides record attempts 5 then 4 and the same tokens; TS sent 1 wire on the retry, Rust 4. probes-4 shows the same on FIRST checks with clean caches (multi 5–6 segments × confidential/5xx: TS 4 classifier wires, Rust 5; both record attempts 5). Mechanism (controller read frozen check.ts:285–310): frozen's worker increments `calls` and starts callSegment for the next segment, but the decisive verdict aborts `stop` before that call reaches fetch, so frozen counts a logical call it never sends; Rust's refilled future is polled immediately and the request leaves. docs/smart-router-compatibility.md's "upstream requests unchanged" is therefore unsupported.
Decision needed from the user: accept a wider documented scheduling class (extra/fewer classifier requests actually sent to the configured local classifier around a decisive verdict; never a different destination, never confidential text to an external provider; client response and routing identical) or attempt to reproduce frozen's start-to-send gap (likely still timing dependent).

USER RULING (C1 scheduling, Nov 16, "Terima sebagai perbedaan"): ACCEPTED documented difference — around the first decisive classifier verdict, the number of classifier requests actually sent (e.g. 4 vs 5, first check or retry), the aggregated classifier row's attempts/tokens/cost, and the cache warmth (hence later checks' request counts) may differ and vary between runs; client response, routing decision, relabel, classifier destination (always the configured local-only classifier), parent row and the classifier row's presence/status are identical. Controller rewrote docs/smart-router-compatibility.md accordingly and committed it on the branch as 243a9343 (docs only).
C1 APPROVED on that basis (reviewer's only remaining finding was the documentation/acceptance scope, now ruled).
